[36muser[0m
Assess this manuscript's publication potential honestly. You are not its author and you gain nothing from flattering it.

Read main.tex and its section files, the artifacts/ verification scripts and their output, and any oracle_sprint_*.md replies in artifacts/ (those are an external expert's rounds, already filtered by a verifier).

Answer these, in order, in ENGLISH, concisely:

1. HEADLINE RESULT. State the single strongest theorem in one sentence, as a mathematician would state it. Give its label.

2. IS IT ACTUALLY NEW? Judge independently. Search arXiv/MathSciNet-style knowledge for the nearest prior work and name it. Then classify the headline as one of:
   - GENUINELY NEW (no prior work does this)
   - INCREMENTAL (a known result extended by a step)
   - REPACKAGING (known content in new notation)
   Justify in two sentences with the specific prior work.

3. DEPTH OF THE MATHEMATICS. Is the proof technique substantial (new mechanism, hard estimate, genuine obstruction overcome), or is it bookkeeping/computation? Be blunt.

4. TOP-JOURNAL VERDICT. Could this, as it stands, plausibly be accepted at a top-tier journal in its field (Annals/Inventiones/JAMS tier = TIER-1; JFA/ETDS/Trans.AMS/Math.Comp tier = TIER-2; solid specialist journal = TIER-3; borderline/niche = TIER-4)? Give ONE tier and one sentence of justification. Do not inflate.

5. WHAT WOULD MOVE IT UP ONE TIER. The single most valuable missing theorem or strengthening, stated precisely. If nothing realistic would move it up, say so.

6. WEAKEST LINK. The claim most likely to draw a referee's objection, and why.

Be concise: at most 30 lines total. No praise, no hedging filler.

NOTE: this manuscript was revised recently. Judge the CURRENT text only. Do not assume earlier assessments were right, and do not look for reasons to agree with them.

## Instruction Priority

Superpowers skills override default system prompt behavior, but **user instructions always take precedence**:

1. **User's explicit instructions** (CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, AGENTS.md, direct requests) â€” highest priority
2. **Superpowers skills** â€” override default system behavior where they conflict
3. **Default system prompt** â€” lowest priority

If CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md, or AGENTS.md says "don't use TDD" and a skill says "always use TDD," follow the user's instructions. The user is in control.

## How to Access Skills

**In Claude Code:** Use the `Skill` tool. When you invoke a skill, its content is loaded and presented to youâ€”follow it directly. Never use the Read tool on skill files.

**In Copilot CLI:** Use the `skill` tool. Skills are auto-discovered from installed plugins. The `skill` tool works the same as Claude Code's `Skill` tool.

**In Gemini CLI:** Skills activate via the `activate_skill` tool. Gemini loads skill metadata at session start and activates the full content on demand.

**In other environments:** Check your platform's documentation for how skills are loaded.

## Platform Adaptation

Skills use Claude Code tool names. Non-CC platforms: see `references/copilot-tools.md` (Copilot CLI), `references/codex-tools.md` (Codex) for tool equivalents. Gemini CLI users get the tool mapping loaded automatically via GEMINI.md.

# Using Skills

## The Rule

**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action.** Even a 1% chance a skill might apply means that you should invoke the skill to check. If an invoked skill turns out to be wrong for the situation, you don't need to use it.

```dot
digraph skill_flow {
    "User message received" [shape=doublecircle];
    "About to EnterPlanMode?" [shape=doublecircle];
    "Already brainstormed?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke brainstorming skill" [shape=box];
    "Might any skill apply?" [shape=diamond];
    "Invoke Skill tool" [shape=box];
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" [shape=box];
    "Has checklist?" [shape=diamond];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [shape=box];
    "Follow skill exactly" [shape=box];
    "Respond (including clarifications)" [shape=doublecircle];

    "About to EnterPlanMode?" -> "Already brainstormed?";
    "Already brainstormed?" -> "Invoke brainstorming skill" [label="no"];
    "Already brainstormed?" -> "Might any skill apply?" [label="yes"];
    "Invoke brainstorming skill" -> "Might any skill apply?";

    "User message received" -> "Might any skill apply?";
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Invoke Skill tool" [label="yes, even 1%"];
    "Might any skill apply?" -> "Respond (including clarifications)" [label="definitely not"];
    "Invoke Skill tool" -> "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'";
    "Announce: 'Using [skill] to [purpose]'" -> "Has checklist?";
    "Has checklist?" -> "Create TodoWrite todo per item" [label="yes"];
    "Has checklist?" -> "Follow skill exactly" [label="no"];
    "Create TodoWrite todo per item" -> "Follow skill exactly";
}
```

## Red Flags

These thoughts mean STOPâ€”you're rationalizing:

| Thought | Reality |
|---------|---------|
| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept â‰  using the skill. Invoke it. |

## Skill Priority

When multiple skills could apply, use this order:

1. **Process skills first** (brainstorming, debugging) - these determine HOW to approach the task
2. **Implementation skills second** (frontend-design, mcp-builder) - these guide execution

"Let's build X" â†’ brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
"Fix this bug" â†’ debugging first, then domain-specific skills.

## Skill Types

**Rigid** (TDD, debugging): Follow exactly. Don't adapt away discipline.

**Flexible** (patterns): Adapt principles to context.

The skill itself tells you which.

## User Instructions

Instructions say WHAT, not HOW. "Add X" or "Fix Y" doesn't mean skip workflows.

\title[Cayley--Poisson and Stable-Kernel Entropy]{Cayley--Poisson Entropy and
Optimal Stable-Kernel Moment Exponents}
\author{Anonymous author}
\date{}

\keywords{Cayley transform, Poisson semigroup, isotropic stable kernel,
relative entropy asymptotics, optimal moment exponent, Cayley--Chebyshev
modes, nonlocal Bregman dissipation, regular variation}
\subjclass[2020]{47D07, 60E10, 94A17, 42A38}

\def\JFAArticleMain{1}
\begin{abstract}
For a probability law \(\nu\) on \(\RR\), the Cayley transform reduces the
large-scale matched Poisson entropy to finite Laurent constant-term algebra.
Writing \(X_c=\gamma-\bar\gamma\), we prove the unconditional threshold
\[
\mathbb E X_c^2<\infty
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\limsup_{t\to\infty}t^4
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_t*\nu\,\|\,P_t(\cdot-\bar\gamma))<\infty,
\]
with limit \((\mathbb E X_c^2)^2/8\) in the finite-variance case and
\(+\infty\) otherwise.  More generally, finite centred \((2N-2)\)th absolute
moment yields an expansion through \(t^{-2N}\), with all odd coefficients
zero and every even coefficient given by one Cayley--Chebyshev constant-term
rule.  For \(m\ge3\), its top layer is
\(\kappa_m\mathfrak m_2\mathfrak m_{2m-2}\), where
\(\kappa_m=(-1)^m(m-1)2^{-2m+2}\ne0\); hence the \((2m-2)\)nd absolute moment
is the sharp universal polynomial-evaluability threshold.  The resulting
coefficient defects form an explicit two-level ladder.

At a missing top moment, if the centred tails are two-sided regularly varying
of index \(-(2N-2)\), with common slowly varying factor \(L\) and
\(\int^tL(s)\dd s/s\to\infty\), then, without symmetry, a density assumption,
or monotonicity of \(L\),
\[
\frac{t^{2N}}{M_{2N-2}(t)}
\left\{D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_t*\nu\,\|\,P_t)
-\sum_{j=2}^{N-1}A_{2j}(\nu)t^{-2j}\right\}
\longrightarrow \kappa_N\mu_2.
\]
Gaussian-quadrature comparison further gives unconditional all-order moment
thresholds and positive square boundary terms under regular variation.

In \(\RR^d\), finite \(L\)-moment with \(L\ge d+1\) produces the matched
radial quotient jet and tensor KL coefficients through order \(L+2\), with a
sharp two-region remainder criterion.  The sharp all-class
\(L^2(\Omega_d)\) exponent is \(\max\{2,(d+1)/2\}\).  Finite covariance is
equivalent to the leading Poisson KL coefficient for \(d\le3\).  More
generally, for every isotropic strictly \(\alpha\)-stable kernel,
\(0<\alpha<2\), the optimal uniform sufficient absolute-moment exponent for
the covariance-order KL asymptotic is
\[
 p_{\alpha,d}=\max\left\{2,
 \frac{4(d+\alpha)}{d+\alpha+4}\right\}.
\]
The endpoint is sufficient and every smaller exponent fails uniformly over
its moment class.  This is not a law-by-law necessity assertion when
\(d+\alpha>4\).  In the Poisson case, multivariate regular variation alone
does not determine the leading entropy; a covariance-matched proxy isolates
the obstruction in an asymptotically exact nonnegative defect and admits a
raw-tail Poisson-energy decomposition.

Finally, the secant modes generate a Poisson-strip RKHS with an explicit Gram
kernel, unitary Poisson image, integer-orbit symbol, and exact lattice
projection theorem for observation channels.  Under finite variance the
moving Cauchy quotient also satisfies the fully justified nonlocal Bregman
tail identity
\[
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_t*\nu\,\|\,P_t(\cdot-\bar\gamma))
=\int_t^\infty\mathcal I_\nu(s)\,\dd s.
\]
\end{abstract}

# Theorem Inventory

Stage A scope is led by the sharp moment-characterization hierarchy for the
universal Poisson entropy coefficients and by the unconditional analytic
variance threshold.  The all-order Cayley-Chebyshev coefficient theorem,
finite-moment and compact-support remainder interfaces, sixth/eighth defect
consequences, Doob/Phi dissipation identity, and Poisson strip RKHS
integer-orbit sampling theorem are supporting packages.  The finite
eighth-order formula is present as a corollary, not as the endpoint theorem.

## In-Scope Core Present

- `thm:sharp-moment-equivalence-hierarchy` in `sec_entropy_core_main.tex`: for
  every `m >= 2`, canonical finiteness of `A_{2m}(nu)` is equivalent to the
  exact finite centred absolute moment of order `2m-2`; the proof computes the
  nonzero top Cayley-mode coefficient and supplies a lower-moment
  counterexample at every order.  No tail class is assumed.
- `thm:sharp-leading-kl-variance-threshold` in
  `sec_entropy_core_main.tex`: finite centred variance iff
  `limsup t^4 H(t) < infinity`, with limit `(E X_c^2)^2/8` in the finite case
  and `+infinity` otherwise.  The converse is a bounded second-mode/Pinsker
  argument, not a Taylor expansion.
- `thm:poisson-kl-even-orders` in `sec_entropy_core_main.tex`: supporting
  all-order finite-moment Poisson KL expansion with only even powers.
- `thm:universal-entropy-coefficients`, `prop:universal-entropy-coefficient-evaluation`, and `cor:universal-entropy-coefficient-package` in `sec_entropy_10_normalization_modes.tex`: universal mode-integral coefficient calculus and finite-moment evaluation.
- `thm:finite-fourier-mode-integral-algorithm` and `cor:below-barrier-finite-grid-eighth-jet`: finite Laurent/Fourier extraction of mode integrals and the eighth-order below-barrier grid specialization.
- `thm:finite-moment-density-remainder`, `thm:finite-moment-entropy-remainder`, and `thm:analytic-compact-support-interchange`: density, finite-moment entropy, and compact-support analytic regimes are separated.
- Remainder infrastructure is present under `lem:entropy-uniform-remainder`, `lem:density-to-entropy-domination`, `lem:finite-moment-density-to-entropy-bounds`, `lem:explicit-entropy-constant-bound`, `lem:moment-ball-uniform-constant-domination`, `lem:finite-moment-entropy-passage`, `lem:strict-finite-moment-entropy-regime`, `cor:uniform-moment-ball-entropy-remainder`, `lem:finite-moment-coefficient-closure`, and `lem:coefficient-regime-separation`.

## Naive Truncation Risks

- `lem:fourth-moment-algebraic-defect`, `lem:fourth-moment-algebraic-coupling`,
  and `lem:seventh-moment-bridge`: fourth-moment algebra must not be treated as
  a genuine entropy coefficient without the seventh-moment bridge.
- `prop:finite-secant-entropy-jet-obstruction`: finite entropy coefficient jets
  do not reconstruct the law.
- `prop:eighth-order-finite-phase-verification`: finite grids are exact
  below-barrier verification devices, not numerical quadrature.
- `thm:poisson-observation-lattice-sampling`: integer samples recover the
  lattice projection; full recovery requires membership in the lattice subspace.

## Split-Paper Material

- `scope:algebraic-phase-spectrum-branch`: circle-dimension rigidity, finite
  abelian group reconstruction, phase-spectrum arithmetic, dyadic half-circle
  laws, anomaly formulas, and injectivization mechanisms belong to a separate
  algebraic paper.
- `scope:general-nonlocal-functional-inequality-branch`: broader nonlocal
  functional-inequality stability generated by the Doob/Bregman identity would
  need separate coercivity and extremizer theory.
- `scope:shifted-nonuniform-lattice-sampling`: shifted, sublattice, and
  nonuniform sampling in the strip RKHS are outside the integer-orbit theorem.
- `scope:finite-state-prime-language-ledger`: DFA, Zeckendorf, prime-language,
  natural-boundary, and finite-zeta obstruction results are unrelated to this
  analytic manuscript and remain only ledger memory.

\title[Cayley--Poisson and Stable-Kernel Entropy]{Cayley--Poisson Entropy and
Optimal Stable-Kernel Moment Exponents}
\author{Anonymous author}
\date{}

\keywords{Cayley transform, Poisson semigroup, isotropic stable kernel,

\begin{document}
\def\JFAArticleMain{1}
\begin{abstract}
For a probability law \(\nu\) on \(\RR\), the Cayley transform reduces the
large-scale matched Poisson entropy to finite Laurent constant-term algebra.
Writing \(X_c=\gamma-\bar\gamma\), we prove the unconditional threshold
\[
\mathbb E X_c^2<\infty
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\limsup_{t\to\infty}t^4
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_t*\nu\,\|\,P_t(\cdot-\bar\gamma))<\infty,
\]
with limit \((\mathbb E X_c^2)^2/8\) in the finite-variance case and
\(+\infty\) otherwise.  More generally, finite centred \((2N-2)\)th absolute
moment yields an expansion through \(t^{-2N}\), with all odd coefficients
zero and every even coefficient given by one Cayley--Chebyshev constant-term
rule.  For \(m\ge3\), its top layer is
\(\kappa_m\mathfrak m_2\mathfrak m_{2m-2}\), where
\(\kappa_m=(-1)^m(m-1)2^{-2m+2}\ne0\); hence the \((2m-2)\)nd absolute moment
is the sharp universal polynomial-evaluability threshold.  The resulting
coefficient defects form an explicit two-level ladder.

At a missing top moment, if the centred tails are two-sided regularly varying
of index \(-(2N-2)\), with common slowly varying factor \(L\) and
\(\int^tL(s)\dd s/s\to\infty\), then, without symmetry, a density assumption,
or monotonicity of \(L\),
\[
\frac{t^{2N}}{M_{2N-2}(t)}
\left\{D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_t*\nu\,\|\,P_t)
-\sum_{j=2}^{N-1}A_{2j}(\nu)t^{-2j}\right\}
\longrightarrow \kappa_N\mu_2.
\]
Gaussian-quadrature comparison further gives unconditional all-order moment
thresholds and positive square boundary terms under regular variation.

In \(\RR^d\), finite \(L\)-moment with \(L\ge d+1\) produces the matched
radial quotient jet and tensor KL coefficients through order \(L+2\), with a
sharp two-region remainder criterion.  The sharp all-class
\(L^2(\Omega_d)\) exponent is \(\max\{2,(d+1)/2\}\).  Finite covariance is
equivalent to the leading Poisson KL coefficient for \(d\le3\).  More
generally, for every isotropic strictly \(\alpha\)-stable kernel,
\(0<\alpha<2\), the optimal uniform sufficient absolute-moment exponent for
the covariance-order KL asymptotic is
\[
 p_{\alpha,d}=\max\left\{2,
 \frac{4(d+\alpha)}{d+\alpha+4}\right\}.
\]
The endpoint is sufficient and every smaller exponent fails uniformly over
its moment class.  This is not a law-by-law necessity assertion when
\(d+\alpha>4\).  In the Poisson case, multivariate regular variation alone
does not determine the leading entropy; a covariance-matched proxy isolates
the obstruction in an asymptotically exact nonnegative defect and admits a
raw-tail Poisson-energy decomposition.

Finally, the secant modes generate a Poisson-strip RKHS with an explicit Gram
kernel, unitary Poisson image, integer-orbit symbol, and exact lattice
projection theorem for observation channels.  Under finite variance the
moving Cauchy quotient also satisfies the fully justified nonlocal Bregman
tail identity
\[
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_t*\nu\,\|\,P_t(\cdot-\bar\gamma))
=\int_t^\infty\mathcal I_\nu(s)\,\dd s.
\]
\end{abstract}

below. The remaining items are exact contextual comparators; their theorems

## Novelty Determination

No arXiv record located by the targeted or comparator queries states the
following combination: a fixed centred input law, harmonic Poisson/Cauchy
smoothing, subtraction of all finite Cayley--entropy coefficients below the
missing moment, a tail index exactly \(2N-2\), and the explicit limit
normalized by
\(\ell_L(t)=\int^tL(s)\,ds/s\).

The nearest results separate into three established bodies of work:
Karamata/de Haan regular-variation theory; de Bruijn or Hardy--Stein entropy
dissipation identities; and entropic convergence to stable laws. The
manuscript cites those inputs and does not claim any of their theorems as
new. Its new statement is the quotient-to-entropy synthesis yielding the
constant
\[
p(c_++c_-)(-1)^N(N-1)2^{-2N+2}\mu_2.
\]

This is a documented negative literature search, not a logically exhaustive
proof that no publication exists outside the indexed sources. Within the
queried arXiv corpus and the standard references/comparators above, no prior
publication of the stated theorem was found.

\]

## Stable-Kernel Critical-Moment Search

Checked 2026-08-08 (Asia/Singapore).  The search target was the optimal
absolute-moment hypothesis for the covariance-order relative-entropy
asymptotic of a fixed law smoothed by an isotropic strictly alpha-stable
kernel, with the stable kernel itself as the translated reference.

The nearest paper was inspected in full:

- K. Ishige, T. Kawakami, and H. Michihisa, *Asymptotic Expansions of
  Solutions of Fractional Diffusion Equations*, SIAM J. Math. Anal. 49
  (2017), 2167--2190, arXiv:1610.09789,
  DOI 10.1137/16M1101428.
- K. Ishige and T. Kawakami, *Refined Asymptotic Expansions of Solutions to
  Fractional Diffusion Equations*, J. Dynam. Differential Equations 36
  (2024), 2679--2702, arXiv:2109.14193,
  DOI 10.1007/s10884-022-10224-4.

Their theorems, bibliographies, and forward-citation records were checked,
along with Crossref, Semantic Scholar, zbMATH, and targeted arXiv searches
for combinations of stable/fractional heat kernels, translated density
quotients, relative entropy, asymptotic expansions, and moment conditions.
The arXiv API returned HTTP 429 during this audit, so arXiv abstract pages,
HTML, and PDFs were used to inspect the relevant records.  Google Scholar
was also queried but returned its unusual-traffic interstitial; no Scholar
results are therefore represented as inspected.

Ishige--Kawakami--Michihisa prove density-level fractional-heat expansions:
moment-weighted kernel derivatives are subtracted and the remainder is
controlled in scaled L^q and weighted L^1 norms.  The refined paper extends
that framework to inhomogeneous and nonlinear equations.  Neither paper
states the critical matched-quotient estimate

\[
\left\|\frac{p_1(\mathord\cdot-z)}{p_1}\right\|_{L^q(p_1)}^q
\asymp (1+|z|)^{(d+\alpha)(q-1)},
\]

uses it to transfer a moment expansion through relative entropy, or gives
the uniform sufficient exponent

\[
p_{\alpha,d}=\max\left\{2,
\frac{4(d+\alpha)}{d+\alpha+4}\right\}.
\]

The stable-entropy papers of Bobkov--Chistyakov--Goetze, Johnson, and
Toscani concern convergence of normalized convolution sequences or entropy
relative to a fixed stable target.  They do not address this fixed-input,
large-spatial-scale, moving matched-reference problem.  No indexed source
located in the audit states the exponent or the matching uniform
moment-class optimality result.  This remains a qualified negative search,
not a proof of absence from every publication.

| label | kind | class | closest-prior | citation-key | increment | hypothesis-status |

# Theorem Inventory

Stage A scope is led by the sharp moment-characterization hierarchy for the
universal Poisson entropy coefficients and by the unconditional analytic
variance threshold.  The all-order Cayley-Chebyshev coefficient theorem,
finite-moment and compact-support remainder interfaces, sixth/eighth defect
consequences, Doob/Phi dissipation identity, and Poisson strip RKHS
integer-orbit sampling theorem are supporting packages.  The finite
eighth-order formula is present as a corollary, not as the endpoint theorem.

## In-Scope Core Present

- `thm:sharp-moment-equivalence-hierarchy` in `sec_entropy_core_main.tex`: for
  every `m >= 2`, canonical finiteness of `A_{2m}(nu)` is equivalent to the
  exact finite centred absolute moment of order `2m-2`; the proof computes the
  nonzero top Cayley-mode coefficient and supplies a lower-moment
  counterexample at every order.  No tail class is assumed.
- `thm:sharp-leading-kl-variance-threshold` in
  `sec_entropy_core_main.tex`: finite centred variance iff
  `limsup t^4 H(t) < infinity`, with limit `(E X_c^2)^2/8` in the finite case
  and `+infinity` otherwise.  The converse is a bounded second-mode/Pinsker
  argument, not a Taylor expansion.
- `thm:poisson-kl-even-orders` in `sec_entropy_core_main.tex`: supporting
  all-order finite-moment Poisson KL expansion with only even powers.
- `thm:universal-entropy-coefficients`, `prop:universal-entropy-coefficient-evaluation`, and `cor:universal-entropy-coefficient-package` in `sec_entropy_10_normalization_modes.tex`: universal mode-integral coefficient calculus and finite-moment evaluation.
- `thm:finite-fourier-mode-integral-algorithm` and `cor:below-barrier-finite-grid-eighth-jet`: finite Laurent/Fourier extraction of mode integrals and the eighth-order below-barrier grid specialization.
- `thm:finite-moment-density-remainder`, `thm:finite-moment-entropy-remainder`, and `thm:analytic-compact-support-interchange`: density, finite-moment entropy, and compact-support analytic regimes are separated.
- Remainder infrastructure is present under `lem:entropy-uniform-remainder`, `lem:density-to-entropy-domination`, `lem:finite-moment-density-to-entropy-bounds`, `lem:explicit-entropy-constant-bound`, `lem:moment-ball-uniform-constant-domination`, `lem:finite-moment-entropy-passage`, `lem:strict-finite-moment-entropy-regime`, `cor:uniform-moment-ball-entropy-remainder`, `lem:finite-moment-coefficient-closure`, and `lem:coefficient-regime-separation`.
- The eighth-order layer is present under `cor:poisson-kl-eighth`, `cor:seventh-moment-eighth-bridge`, `prop:eighth-order-jet-subordinate`, `lem:entropy-coefficient-regime-separation`, `cor:eighth-order-jet-factorization`, and `prop:eighth-order-finite-phase-verification`.
- Defect rigidity and stability are present under `thm:poisson-defect-ladder`, `thm:poisson-defect-amplification`, `cor:poisson-two-point-defect-rigidity`, `thm:poisson-defect-stability`, `cor:poisson-finite-time-defect-stability`, `cor:defect-stability-constant-regimes`, and `cor:uniform-finite-time-defect-stability`.
- Fourth-moment algebra and descent are present under `lem:fourth-moment-algebraic-defect`, `lem:fourth-moment-algebraic-coupling`, and `lem:seventh-moment-bridge`.
- Cayley and finite phase infrastructure are present under `prop:cayley-homeo`, `prop:phase-derivative`, `thm:haar-pullback-uniqueness`, `def:finite-phase-grid`, `prop:phase-coordinate-identification`, `prop:finite-grid-coefficient-recovery`, and `cor:alias-free-cayley-mode-product-quadrature`.
- Poisson normalization and mode calculus are present under `thm:cayley-entropy-identity`, `prop:poisson-identities`, `prop:poisson-semigroup-law`, `prop:poisson-second-order`, `thm:cayley-chebyshev-mode`, `prop:cayley-mode-remainder`, `lem:tail-uniform-cayley-mode-remainder`, `prop:poisson-cauchy-moment-inversion-by-phase-modes`, `thm:finite-grid-poisson-moment-recovery`, and `lem:mode-parity-vanish`.
- Doob/Phi dissipation is present under `lem:cauchy-doob-cutoff-calculus`, `lem:doob-range-bregman-integrability`, `lem:compact-range-phi-approximation`, `lem:doob-bregman-domain-bounds`, `lem:poisson-second-moment-kl-decay`, `thm:poisson-doob-phi-entropy`, `cor:poisson-doob-rigidity`, and `cor:poisson-bregman-tail-coefficients`.
- Gram/RKHS and observation-channel results are present under `thm:mode-gram-kernel`, `cor:mode-quadratic-integrals`, `thm:mode-space-rkhs`, `thm:higher-secant-kernels`, `cor:higher-secant-entropy-polynomial`, `prop:higher-secant-subordinate-support`, `prop:finite-secant-entropy-jet-obstruction`, `prop:poisson-central-two-channel`, `cor:poisson-symmetrization`, `cor:poisson-symmetric-case`, and `thm:poisson-channel-rkhs`.
- Strip RKHS and integer-orbit sampling are present under `prop:gram-space-poisson-image`, `prop:poisson-hardy-splitting`, `lem:auxiliary-uniform-lattice-projection`, `cor:integer-orbit-projection`, `prop:integer-orbit-lattice-interface`, `cor:integer-orbit-reconstruction-rigidity`, `prop:integer-orbit-scoped-quotient`, `lem:imported-principal-symbol-sampling`, `prop:poisson-lattice-symbol`, `prop:poisson-lattice-sampling`, `cor:poisson-cardinal-reconstruction`, and `thm:poisson-observation-lattice-sampling`.
- Appendix arithmetic support is present under `lem:entropy-integrals` and `lem:eighth-order-coefficient-arithmetic`.

## Missing Or Weak Core Results

No missing in-scope theorem-like environment was found relative to the current
scope contract and Oracle rerun directive.  No present core result is classified
as too weak for the stated Stage A theorem package.  The finite eighth-order
statement is intentionally a corollary under seventh moments and is not being
used as the main endpoint theorem.

## Proof And Editorial Gaps

- `main-bibliography-usage`: `stage_a_audit.json` still requests a final
  bibliography usage pass across `main.tex` and all TeX citations.
- `literature-audit-stale-labels`: `literature_audit.md` contains stale labels
  from earlier drafts and should be reconciled before review-bundle freeze.

## Supporting Or Background Results

The following theorem-like items are supporting or classical inputs rather than
new central claims: `prop:poisson-identities`, `prop:poisson-semigroup-law`,
`prop:poisson-hardy-splitting`, `lem:auxiliary-uniform-lattice-projection`,
`lem:imported-principal-symbol-sampling`, `lem:entropy-integrals`, and
`lem:eighth-order-coefficient-arithmetic`.  These should remain cited as
background or appendix support.

## Naive Truncation Risks

- `lem:fourth-moment-algebraic-defect`, `lem:fourth-moment-algebraic-coupling`,
  and `lem:seventh-moment-bridge`: fourth-moment algebra must not be treated as
  a genuine entropy coefficient without the seventh-moment bridge.
- `prop:finite-secant-entropy-jet-obstruction`: finite entropy coefficient jets
  do not reconstruct the law.
- `prop:eighth-order-finite-phase-verification`: finite grids are exact
  below-barrier verification devices, not numerical quadrature.
- `thm:poisson-observation-lattice-sampling`: integer samples recover the
  lattice projection; full recovery requires membership in the lattice subspace.

## Split-Paper Material

- `scope:algebraic-phase-spectrum-branch`: circle-dimension rigidity, finite
  abelian group reconstruction, phase-spectrum arithmetic, dyadic half-circle
  laws, anomaly formulas, and injectivization mechanisms belong to a separate
  algebraic paper.
- `scope:general-nonlocal-functional-inequality-branch`: broader nonlocal
  functional-inequality stability generated by the Doob/Bregman identity would
  need separate coercivity and extremizer theory.
- `scope:shifted-nonuniform-lattice-sampling`: shifted, sublattice, and
  nonuniform sampling in the strip RKHS are outside the integer-orbit theorem.
- `scope:finite-state-prime-language-ledger`: DFA, Zeckendorf, prime-language,
  natural-boundary, and finite-zeta obstruction results are unrelated to this
  analytic manuscript and remain only ledger memory.

## Journal Style Gaps

Before submission, run a final bibliography/citation pass, preserve the
included-file theorem order after any section splits, keep background sampling
criteria visibly separated from Poisson-specific computations, and resolve any
section-title or label drift recorded in the scope contract.  Stage A anonymity
in the author block is currently intentional.

% sec_introduction.tex -- Introduction
\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}

The Cayley transform
\[
\iota(x)=\frac{1+\ii x}{1-\ii x}
\]
identifies the one-point compactification of \(\RR\) with \(\TT\), and pulls
normalized Haar measure back to the Cauchy law
\[
\omega(\dd x)=\frac{1}{\pi(1+x^{2})}\dd x=P_1(x)\dd x .
\]
This is the endpoint-normalized form of the classical Cauchy/wrapped-Cauchy
M{\"o}bius correspondence in circular statistics
\cite{KentTyler1988WrappedCauchy,McCullagh1992CauchyModels,
McCullagh1996CauchyMobius,
KatoJones2010MobiusCircle,KatoMcCullagh2020SphereCauchyMobius}.
This normalization makes the fixed-law Poisson comparison
\begin{equation}\label{eq:intro-main-functional}
D_{\mathrm{KL}}\bigl(P_t*\nu\,\|\,P_t(\cdot-\bar{\gamma})\bigr)
\end{equation}
the object whose large-\(t\) behaviour is studied in Cayley--Chebyshev
modes.  The input law \(\nu\) is fixed, \(\bar{\gamma}\) is its mean, and
\(t\to\infty\) is the Poisson scale.

The basic calculation is a quotient calculation.  Let
\[
X_c=\gamma-\bar{\gamma}
\]
be the centred translation variable.  With \(x=\bar{\gamma}+ty\), translation
by \(X_c\) is encoded by
\[
R_\varepsilon(y)=\frac{1+y^2}{1+(y-\varepsilon)^2},
\qquad \varepsilon=X_c/t .
\]
The Cayley angle \(y=\tan\theta\) turns the Taylor coefficients of
\(R_\varepsilon\) into finite trigonometric, equivalently Laurent, modes.
After the matched Cauchy profile has been divided out, the quotient expansion
is therefore reduced to finite mode algebra; the KL expansion is then the
ordinary Taylor expansion of \((1+u)\log(1+u)-u\) in
\(L^\infty\cap L^1(\omega)\).

\subsection*{Contribution}

The principal unconditional result is
Theorem~\ref{thm:sharp-leading-kl-variance-threshold}.  For every law with
finite mean and without a tail-class assumption, finite centred variance is
equivalent to bounded \(t^4D_{\rm KL}\); the limit is \(\mu_2^2/8\) in the
finite-variance case and \(+\infty\) otherwise.  The infinite-variance proof
pairs the quotient with the bounded second Cayley mode and therefore does not
use a formal Taylor expansion.

The other principal analytic result is the regularly varying boundary theorem,
Theorem~\ref{cor:main-body-conditional-missing-top-moment-tail}.  For \(N\ge3\),
a centred law with two-sided regularly varying tails of index \(p=2N-2\) and
divergent integrated slowly varying factor
\(\ell_L(t)=\int^tL(s)\dd s/s\) satisfies
\[
t^{2N}\left\{H(t)-\sum_{j=2}^{N-1}A_{2j}(\nu)t^{-2j}\right\}
\sim p(c_++c_-)\kappa_N\mu_2\ell_L(t),
\qquad
\kappa_N=(-1)^N(N-1)2^{-2N+2}.
\]
Equivalently, the residual normalized by the truncated boundary moment has
limit \(\kappa_N\mu_2\).  This is a class-conditional analytic statement,
without a density, symmetry, or monotonicity assumption; it is not an
unconditional higher-order converse.

The all-order coefficient algebra has a different logical status.  For every
\(m\ge3\), the reduced universal polynomial satisfies
\[
A_{2m}^{\rm form}
=\kappa_m\mathfrak m_2\mathfrak m_{2m-2}
+P_m(\mathfrak m_2,\ldots,\mathfrak m_{2m-3}),
\qquad \kappa_m=(-1)^m(m-1)2^{-2m+2}\ne0.
\]
At \(m=2\), one instead has \(A_4^{\rm form}=\mathfrak m_2^2/8\).
Thus \(\EE|X_c|^{2m-2}<\infty\) is sufficient to evaluate every monomial and
is the minimal absolute-moment assumption that guarantees such evaluation
uniformly over all centred laws.  Proposition~\ref{thm:sharp-moment-equivalence-hierarchy}
is only this algebraic polynomial-domain minimality statement.  In particular,
for \(m\ge3\) it is not an unconditional converse for the entropy remainder.

The second theorem is multidimensional and finite-moment.  For
\(L\ge d+1\), finite centred \(L\)-moment closes the large-translation
remainder in the matched radial Poisson quotient, gives an explicit
\(L^\infty\cap L^1(\Omega_d)\) jet, supplies the large-\(t\)
entropy-existence package, and hence gives the tensor KL expansion through
order \(L+2\).  The two-region expression is retained as the quantitative
remainder bound; in this range the precise statement is the equivalence
\(\mathcal R_{d,L}(t)=o(t^{-L})\) for the two-region functional defined in
\path{sec_entropy_core_main.tex}, Definition ``Two-region remainder
functional,'' and the finite
\(L\)-moment assumption.
The restriction \(L\ge d+1\) is sharp for this \(L^\infty\) quotient
topology, as shown by the spike construction below.  It is not claimed to be
necessary for the leading KL coefficient in a weaker \(L^2(\Omega_d)\) or
entropy topology.  Section~\ref{sec:verified-A2-results} treats those weaker
topologies separately: the sharp moment exponent for the \(L^2\) quotient is
\(\max\{2,(d+1)/2\}\); finite covariance is the exact leading-KL threshold
for \(d\le3\); and finite covariance fails to control the leading KL scale
for every \(d\ge4\).  For every isotropic strictly \(\alpha\)-stable
kernel, the optimal uniform sufficient exponent among finite
absolute-moment assumptions is
\[
 p_{\alpha,d}=\max\left\{2,
 \frac{4(d+\alpha)}{d+\alpha+4}\right\},
\]
with the endpoint on the sufficient side; see
Theorem~\ref{thm:high-dimensional-kl-moment-threshold}.  This optimization
over moment classes is not a law-by-law necessity statement when
\(d+\alpha>4\).  The infinite
critical-moment boundary is genuinely different: Theorem
\ref{thm:critical-vague-tail-nonidentifiability} constructs two laws with the
same smooth multivariate regularly varying tail data and the same covariance,
one attaining the covariance coefficient and the other having unbounded
scaled entropy along a subsequence.
Theorem~\ref{thm:covariance-proxy-defect-decomposition} gives an
asymptotically exact
finite-covariance characterization of this distinction.  If
\(k_{\Sigma,t}\) denotes the positive Poisson density obtained by adjoining
the normalized quadratic mode \(t^{-2}b_\Sigma\) to the matched reference,
then
\[
 t^4H_d(t)=\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)
 +t^4D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|k_{\Sigma,t})+o(1).
\]
Thus attainment of the covariance coefficient is equivalent to the
vanishing of the complete nonlinear proxy defect.  This statement requires
only finite covariance and does not impose a density or tail regularity on
the original law.
The leading multidimensional covariance formula is deliberately read through
this analytic route.  Finite covariance alone determines the bounded quadratic
mode \(b_\Sigma\) and the algebraic number \(\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)\), but it
does not by itself make \(\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)\) the coefficient of
\eqref{eq:intro-main-functional}.  It becomes the leading KL coefficient only
after a quotient/existence bridge is in force, for example the
\((\mathrm{MPJ}_{d,L})+(\mathrm{EX}_{d,L})\) package supplied here by
finite \(L\)-moment with \(L\ge d+1\), or the separate second-order quotient
criterion stated in Section~\ref{sec:entropy-asymptotics}.

The nearest fractional-diffusion prior art is
Ishige--Kawakami--Michihisa
\cite{IshigeKawakamiMichihisa2017} and the refined follow-up
\cite{IshigeKawakamiMichihisa2022RefinedFractional}.  For every
\(0<\alpha<2\) and weighted moment order \(K\ge0\), those works subtract
moment-weighted derivatives of the stable heat kernel and prove scaled
\(L^q\) and weighted \(L^1\) remainder decay for the resulting density
expansion, including inhomogeneous and nonlinear refinements.  They do not
control the matched density quotient in
\(L^{q_{\alpha,d}}(p_1^{(\alpha,d)}\dd y)\) or transfer that critical
estimate through relative entropy; those are the steps that produce
\(p_{\alpha,d}\) here.

\ifdefined\JFAArticleMain
The full domain-closure motivation and comparison with the general
two-solution Bregman calculus are in \path{sec_introduction.tex}, the
paragraphs preceding ``Theorems,'' collected by
\path{supplement_relocated_support.tex}.
\else
The third theorem is a domain-and-endpoint result for the standard
simultaneous-semigroup relative-entropy calculus.  For a symmetric jump
generator
\[
(L_Jf)(x)=\int\{f(y)-f(x)\}J(x,y)\,\dd y,
\qquad J(x,y)=J(y,x),
\]
and sufficiently regular positive solutions
\(\partial_t h_t=L_Jh_t\), \(\partial_t g_t=L_Jg_t\), set
\(u_t=h_t/g_t\).  The general generator identity is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:intro-general-two-solution-generator-identity}
-\frac{\dd}{\dd t}D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|g_t)
=\frac12\iint J(x,y)
\left[g_t(x)\Lambda(u_t(x),u_t(y))
+g_t(y)\Lambda(u_t(y),u_t(x))\right]\dd x\dd y.
\end{equation}
By exchanging \(x\) and \(y\) in the second term, the right side equals
\[
\iint J(x,y)g_t(x)\Lambda(u_t(x),u_t(y))\dd x\dd y.
\]
This is the logarithmic relative-\(\Phi\)-entropy identity for two solutions
of the same symmetric jump semigroup; its Bregman differentiation is not
claimed as new \cite{Chafai2004PhiEntropies,BakryCoulhonLedouxSaloffCoste1995}.

Under finite centred variance, Theorem~\ref{thm:main-C} verifies that the
moving matched Cauchy quotient lies in the domain of this identity on every
compact time window, with the generator normalized as the symmetric
\(1\)-stable Cauchy generator
\cite{Feller1971,Bertoin1996LevyProcesses,Sato1999LevyProcesses,
Applebaum2009LevyProcesses,Kwasnicki2017}.  The KL functional is then the
tail integral of the resulting nonlocal dissipation.  Here both the quotient
\[
u_t=\frac{h_t}{g_t}
\]
and the reference density \(g_t=P_t(\cdot-\bar\gamma)\) move with \(t\).
The logarithmic chain rule therefore first gives the two-pair identity
\begin{equation}\label{eq:intro-two-pair-chain-rule}
H'(t)=\langle \log u_t,\partial_t h_t\rangle
      -\langle u_t-1,\partial_t g_t\rangle,
\qquad H(t)=D_{\rm KL}(h_t\|g_t),
\end{equation}
not a single fixed-reference Dirichlet form.  Applying the Cauchy Green
formula separately to the pairs \((h_t,\log u_t)\) and \((g_t,u_t)\), and
using \(\int\partial_tg_t=0\), produces the algebraic difference
\[
(\rho_xa-\rho_yb)(\log a-\log b)-(\rho_x-\rho_y)(a-b)
=\rho_x\Lambda(a,b)+\rho_y\Lambda(b,a),
\]
where \(a=u_t(x)\), \(b=u_t(y)\), \(\rho_x=g_t(x)\),
\(\rho_y=g_t(y)\), and
\(\Lambda(a,b)=a\log(a/b)-a+b\).  This is the logarithmic case of the
Csisz{\'a}r--Morimoto--Ali--Silvey \(f\)-divergence/Bregman calculus
\cite{AliSilvey1966Divergence,Csiszar1967,Morimoto1963Markov,
Vajda1989TheoryDivergence,Bregman1967}.  The actual increment is to justify
these formal pairings on the noncompact Cauchy state space: finite variance
yields compact-window upper and lower quotient bounds, valid Green cutoffs,
and integrable domination of the Bregman kernel.  The separate endpoint proof
\(H(T)\to0\) then permits integration to infinity.

The coefficient-domain and boundary-layer increments are separate from
that dissipation identity.  Stable-semigroup de Bruijn and Hardy--Stein formulas identify an
entropy derivative or an integrated Bregman/Dirichlet form
\cite{DeBruijn1953,HirataNemotoYoshida2012RelativeEntropy,
Yoshida2017RelativeEntropyDiffusion,BanuelosBogdanLuks2016HardyStein,
Gutowski2023HardySteinPureJump,
BogdanGutowskiPietruskaPaluba2025PolarizedHardyStein,
Johnson2013StableDeBruijn,Toscani2016StableEntropy,
BobkovChistyakovGotze2013StableEntropy}.  They do not determine the large-time
coefficient residual for a fixed input law against a moving matched Cauchy
reference.  The analytic increments here are the unconditional bounded-mode
converse at the variance threshold and the signed,
truncated-moment-normalized residual within the stated regularly varying
class.  The all-order Cayley--Laurent calculation instead supplies a finite
polynomial-evaluation algorithm and its minimal universal absolute-moment
assumption.
\fi

\subsection*{Theorems}\label{subsec:main-results}

\begin{maintheorem}{Analytic variance threshold and coefficient-domain minimality}
\label{thm:main-A}
Let \(\nu\) be a probability law on \(\RR\) with finite mean
\(\bar\gamma\), and put \(X_c=\gamma-\bar\gamma\).  No variance or tail
assumption is imposed at this point.  Set
\[
H(t)=D_{\mathrm{KL}}\bigl(P_t*\nu\,\|\,P_t(\cdot-\bar\gamma)\bigr)
\]
with the extended nonnegative convention for relative entropy.  Then
\[
\EE X_c^2<\infty
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\limsup_{t\to\infty}t^4H(t)<\infty .
\]
If the variance is finite, then \(H(t)<\infty\) for every \(t>0\) and
\[
\lim_{t\to\infty}t^4H(t)=\frac{(\EE X_c^2)^2}{8};
\]
if it is infinite, then
\[
\lim_{t\to\infty}t^4H(t)=+\infty
\]
in the extended sense.  No compact-support, exponential-tail, or
regular-variation hypothesis is used in this equivalence.

For the higher universal coefficients, the conclusion is algebraic rather
than an unconditional analytic converse.  For every \(m\ge3\), let
\(A_{2m}^{\rm form}\) be the reduced universal polynomial defined by the
constant-term rule below.  Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:main-A-moment-equivalence}
A_{2m}^{\rm form}
=\kappa_m\mathfrak m_2\mathfrak m_{2m-2}
+P_m(\mathfrak m_2,\ldots,\mathfrak m_{2m-3}),
\qquad
\kappa_m=(-1)^m(m-1)2^{-2m+2}\ne0,
\end{equation}
At \(m=2\), \(A_4^{\rm form}=\mathfrak m_2^2/8\).  Consequently, for every
\(m\ge2\),
\(\EE|X_c|^{2m-2}<\infty\) is sufficient for the canonical Lebesgue
evaluation of every monomial, and is minimal among absolute-moment
assumptions that guarantee evaluation uniformly over all centred probability
laws.  This statement uses the deliberately specified polynomial-evaluation
domain of Definition~\ref{def:canonical-coefficient-finiteness}; for
\(m\ge3\) it is not an unconditional converse for the entropy remainder.

The supporting coefficient expansion has the exact moment hypothesis as
follows.  Let \(N\ge2\), put \(L=2N-2\), and assume now that
\(\EE|X_c|^L<\infty\).  With
\[
\begin{aligned}
  \mu_n&=\int_{\RR} X_c^n\,\dd\nu(\gamma)\qquad(2\le n\le L),\\
  h_t&=P_t*\nu,\qquad
  g_t&=P_t(\cdot-\bar{\gamma}),
\end{aligned}
\]
one has
\[
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(h_t\|g_t)
=\sum_{m=2}^{N}A_{2m}(\nu)t^{-2m}+o(t^{-2N}).
\]
The coefficients are part of the statement.  For \(2\le m\le N\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:main-A-intro-coefficients}
A_{2m}(\nu)=
\sum_{r=2}^{m}
\frac{(-1)^r}{r(r-1)}
\sum_{\substack{n_1+\cdots+n_r=2m\\ n_j\ge2}}
\mu_{n_1}\cdots\mu_{n_r}
\,[z^0]\prod_{j=1}^r Q_{n_j}(z),
\end{equation}
where \([z^0]\) denotes constant Laurent coefficient and the
Cayley--Chebyshev Laurent representative is fixed by the convention
\begin{equation}\label{eq:main-A-intro-Qn}
Q_n(z)=2^{-n}(-\ii)^n
\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\binom{n-1}{\ell-1}
\bigl(z^\ell+(-1)^nz^{-\ell}\bigr)
\qquad(n\ge1).
\end{equation}
Thus \(A_{2m}(\nu)\) is a universal polynomial in
\(\mu_2,\ldots,\mu_{2m-2}\); the proof that
\eqref{eq:main-A-intro-coefficients} is the entropy coefficient is supplied
by Theorem~\ref{thm:poisson-kl-even-orders}.  All retained odd coefficients
vanish, and the leading coefficient is
\[
A_4(\nu)=\frac{\mu_2^2}{8}.
\]
\end{maintheorem}

\begin{maintheorem}{Multidimensional Poisson jets and stable-kernel moment exponent}
\label{thm:main-B}
Let \(d\ge1\), let \(L\in\NN\) satisfy \(L\ge d+1\), and let \(\nu\) be a
probability measure on \(\RR^d\) with mean \(\bar\gamma\).  Put
\[
X_c=\gamma-\bar\gamma .
\]
Assume \(\EE |X_c|^L<\infty\).  Then the centred moments
\[
\mu_\alpha=\EE[X_c^\alpha]\qquad(|\alpha|\le L)
\]
are finite.  The explicit two-region condition used in the quotient proof is
only the following one:
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal R_{d,L}(t)
&:=t^{-(L+1)}\EE\!\left[
|X_c|^{L+1}{\bf1}_{\{|X_c|\le t\}}\right] \\
&\quad{}+ t^{-L}\EE\!\left[
|X_c|^L{\bf1}_{\{|X_c|>t\}}\right],
\end{aligned}
\]
one has, in the stated range \(L\ge d+1\),
\[
\mathcal R_{d,L}(t)=o(t^{-L})
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\EE |X_c|^L<\infty .
\]
No broader equivalence between arbitrary two-region estimates and moment
hypotheses is asserted.  The restriction \(L\ge d+1\) is sharp for this
\(L^\infty\) quotient topology: for every integer \(1\le L<d+1\), the spike
construction following Theorem~\ref{thm:second-order-quotient-kl-coefficient}
gives a centred law with finite \(L\)th absolute moment whose quotient does
not converge uniformly to one.  This does not establish necessity for a
leading KL coefficient under a weaker \(L^2(\Omega_d)\) or entropy topology;
the separate sharp \(L^2\) result and the dimension-dependent KL statements
are Theorems~\ref{thm:multidim-l2-moment-threshold} and
\ref{thm:low-dimensional-finite-covariance-threshold}, together with
Proposition~\ref{prop:high-dimensional-finite-covariance-obstruction} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:high-dimensional-kl-moment-threshold}.
With
\[
h_t=P_t^{(d)}*\nu,\qquad
g_t=P_t^{(d)}(\cdot-\bar\gamma),
\]
one has, for all sufficiently large \(t\),
\[
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(h_t\|g_t)
=\sum_{m=4}^{L+2}\mathcal A_m^{(d)}(\nu)t^{-m}
+o(t^{-(L+2)}),
\]
where, for \(4\le m\le L+2\),
\[
\mathcal A_m^{(d)}(\nu)=
\sum_{r=2}^{\lfloor m/2\rfloor}
\frac{(-1)^r}{r(r-1)}
\sum_{\substack{\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_r\in\NN_0^d\\
|\alpha_1|+\cdots+|\alpha_r|=m\\
2\le|\alpha_j|\le L\ \mathrm{for}\ 1\le j\le r}}
\mu_{\alpha_1}\cdots\mu_{\alpha_r}
\int_{\RR^d}\prod_{j=1}^r
U_{\alpha_j}^{(d)}(y)\,\Omega_d(\dd y).
\]
Here \(U_\alpha^{(d)}\) denotes the radial Poisson Taylor mode and
\[
\Omega_d(\dd y)
=\frac{\Gamma((d+1)/2)}{\pi^{(d+1)/2}}
(1+|y|^2)^{-(d+1)/2}\,\dd y
\]
is the \(t=1\) \(d\)-dimensional Poisson law.  The displayed formula is a
finite universal tensor polynomial in the centred moments of orders
\(2,\ldots,m-2\), and all retained odd coefficients vanish.
For the leading retained coefficient, let
\[
\Sigma=\EE[X_cX_c^{\mathsf T}],\qquad
\Sigma_0=\Sigma-\frac{\operatorname{tr}\Sigma}{d}I_d .
\]
Then
\[
\mathcal A_4^{(d)}(\nu)=\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)
=c_d^{\rm iso}(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma)^2
+c_d^{\rm tr}\|\Sigma_0\|_{\rm HS}^2,
\]
with
\[
c_d^{\rm iso}=
\frac{3(d+1)(7d+9)}
{4d(d+3)(d+5)(d+7)},
\qquad
c_d^{\rm tr}=
\frac{3(d+1)(d+3)}
{4(d+5)(d+7)} .
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)
=\frac12\int_{\RR^d}b_\Sigma(y)^2\,\Omega_d(\dd y),
\quad
b_\Sigma(y)=
\frac{d+1}{2}\left(
-\frac{\operatorname{tr}\Sigma}{1+|y|^2}
+\frac{(d+3)y^{\mathsf T}\Sigma y}{(1+|y|^2)^2}
\right).
\]
Separately, let \(0<\alpha<2\) and let
\(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}(x)=s^{-d}p_1^{(\alpha,d)}(x/s)\) be any isotropic
strictly \(\alpha\)-stable kernel.  If
\[
 \EE|X_c|^{p_{\alpha,d}}<\infty,
 \qquad
 p_{\alpha,d}=\max\left\{2,
 \frac{4(d+\alpha)}{d+\alpha+4}\right\},
\]
then
\[
 D_{\rm KL}\!\left(p_s^{(\alpha,d)}*\nu\,\middle\|\,
 p_s^{(\alpha,d)}(\mathord\cdot-\bar\gamma)\right)
 =\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d}(\Sigma)s^{-4}+o(s^{-4}),
\]
with \(\mathcal Q_{\alpha,d}\) defined in
\eqref{eq:stable-leading-covariance-functional}.  The exponent is optimal
as a uniform sufficient finite-moment hypothesis, including its endpoint;
no law-by-law necessity is asserted when \(d+\alpha>4\).
\end{maintheorem}

\begin{maintheorem}{Domain and endpoint closure for Cauchy relative entropy}
\label{thm:main-C}
Let \(\nu\) be a probability measure on \(\RR\) with mean \(\bar{\gamma}\) and
finite centred second moment.  Put
\[
\begin{aligned}
h_t&=P_t*\nu,\\
g_t&=P_t(\cdot-\bar{\gamma}),\\
u_t&=h_t/g_t .
\end{aligned}
\]
Define the Bregman kernel by
\[
\Lambda(\alpha,\beta)=\alpha\log\frac{\alpha}{\beta}-\alpha+\beta,
\]
and define the nonlocal Bregman dissipation by the nonnegative Lebesgue
integral
\[
\mathcal I_\nu(t)
=\frac1\pi\iint_{\RR^2}
\frac{g_t(x)\Lambda(u_t(x),u_t(y))}{(x-y)^2}\dd x\dd y ,
\]
with the diagonal ignored.  For the Cauchy jump kernel
\(J(x,y)=\{\pi(x-y)^2\}^{-1}\), this is exactly the right side of the
general two-solution identity displayed in \path{sec_introduction.tex}, in the
paragraphs preceding ``Theorems,'' under the label
\path{eq:intro-general-two-solution-generator-identity}.  The assertion is
that the matched quotient belongs to the required domain: \(\mathcal I_\nu\)
is measurable and locally integrable on \((0,\infty)\).  More precisely, on every compact window
\([a,b]\Subset(0,\infty)\), the quotient \(u_t\) satisfies the bounded
Lipschitz estimates, Green-cutoff convergence, and integrable Bregman-kernel
domination recorded in
\path{sec_doob_phi_entropy.tex}, Proposition ``Matched-Poisson domain and
differentiation package.''  These bounds
justify the general generator identity, while the separate endpoint result
\(D_{\rm KL}(h_T\|g_T)\to0\) justifies passage from a finite time window to
infinity.  Consequently, for every \(t>0\),
\[
D_{\mathrm{KL}}(h_t\|g_t)=\int_t^\infty \mathcal I_\nu(s)\,\dd s .
\]
\end{maintheorem}

\ifdefined\JFAArticleMain
The comparison by mechanism, hypothesis ledger, and detailed organization
guide are in \path{sec_introduction.tex}, after the three main-theorem
statements, collected by \path{supplement_relocated_support.tex}.
\else
\subsection*{Comparison by mechanism}
\label{subsec:intro-recent-jfa-comparison}

Classical moment decompositions expand a function or convolution solution
into masses and derivatives of a fundamental kernel; see
Duoandikoetxea--Zuazua \cite{DuoandikoetxeaZuazua1992} and the subsequent
large-time diffusion literature
\cite{EscobedoZuazua1991,IshigeKawakamiMichihisa2017,
IshigeKawakamiMichihisa2022RefinedFractional}.  In particular,
Ishige--Kawakami--Michihisa treat every \(0<\alpha<2\) and every weighted
moment order \(K\ge0\): for data in
\(L^1(\RR^d,(1+|x|)^K\dd x)\), their Theorem~1.1 subtracts the
moment-weighted derivatives of the fractional heat kernel and proves the
corresponding scaled \(L^q\) and weighted \(L^1\) remainder decay, including
the range \(K\ge\alpha\) where the stable semigroup does not preserve that
weighted space.  The refined follow-up also expands the inhomogeneous and
nonlinear fractional-diffusion solutions.  Those results supply the
density-level moment expansion.  They do not estimate the matched quotient
in the critical space \(L^{q_{\alpha,d}}(p_1^{(\alpha,d)}\dd y)\), transfer
it through the nonlinear KL functional, or identify the optimal uniform
moment exponent above.  These are the increments in
Theorem~\ref{thm:main-B}.

Relative \(\Phi\)-entropy and pure-jump semigroup calculus already provide
the formal two-solution identity
\eqref{eq:intro-general-two-solution-generator-identity}; see Chafa{\"\i}
\cite{Chafai2004PhiEntropies} and, for early nonlinear/nonlocal energy forms,
Bakry--Coulhon--Ledoux--Saloff-Coste
\cite{BakryCoulhonLedouxSaloffCoste1995}.  Theorem~\ref{thm:main-C} therefore
claims only the finite-variance domain verification for the noncompact Cauchy
kernel and the endpoint passage that converts the window identity into a
tail integral.

At formula level, the polarized Hardy--Stein identity
\cite{BogdanGutowskiPietruskaPaluba2025PolarizedHardyStein} states, for its
symmetric Markov semigroup and \(p\ge2\),
\[
\int f g^{\langle p-1\rangle}\dd x
=\int_0^\infty\!\iint
\mathcal J_p(P_s\Phi(x),P_s\Phi(y))J(x,y)\dd x\dd y\dd s,
\qquad \Phi=(f,g),
\]
where, for \(w,z\in\RR^2\),
\[
\mathcal J_p(w,z)=z_1z_2^{\langle p-1\rangle}
-w_1w_2^{\langle p-1\rangle}-w_2^{\langle p-1\rangle}(z_1-w_1)
-(p-1)w_1|w_2|^{p-2}(z_2-w_2).
\]
This is a fixed power pairing.  It does not specialize directly to
\[
\int h_t\log(h_t/g_t)\dd x,
\]
nor does its \(p\ge2\) statement supply the logarithmic endpoint.  More
importantly for the present theorem,
it does not by specialization verify the compact-window bounds and noncompact
Green pairings for \(h_t/g_t\), or prove \(H(T)\to0\).  Those are precisely
the conclusions isolated in Theorem~\ref{thm:main-C}.  A broader comparison
by mechanism is given in Appendix~\ref{app:related-work-scope}.

\subsection*{Hypothesis ledger}
\label{subsec:intro-hypothesis-ledger}

The theorem statements above are intended to be read with the following
minimal hypothesis map.

\begin{center}
{\small
\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\begin{tabularx}{\linewidth}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.20\linewidth}|%
>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.30\linewidth}|%
>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}X}
\textbf{result}
& \textbf{analytic hypothesis}
& \textbf{what it supplies}\\
\hline
Theorem~\ref{thm:main-A}, polynomial-domain minimality
& finite mean; the coefficient domain is the canonical polynomial-evaluation
domain of Definition~\ref{def:canonical-coefficient-finiteness}.
& finite \((2m-2)\)nd absolute moment is the minimal assumption guaranteeing
universal polynomial evaluation; no higher-order analytic converse is
asserted.\\
\hline
Theorem~\ref{thm:main-A}, supporting coefficient expansion
& \(N\ge2\), finite centred \((2N-2)\)nd absolute moment; no stronger tail
class.
& Laurent-mode coefficient expansion through \(t^{-2N}\).\\
\hline
Theorem~\ref{thm:main-A}, threshold
& finite mean for the extended statement; finite variance exactly for the
finite \(t^4\) coefficient.
& finite variance gives \(A_4=(\EE X_c^2)^2/8\); infinite variance gives
\(t^4H(t)\to+\infty\).\\
\hline
Theorem~\ref{cor:main-body-conditional-missing-top-moment-tail}
& \(N\ge3\); centred law with two-sided tails
\(c_\pm t^{-(2N-2)}L(t)\); measurable eventually positive slowly varying
\(L\); \(\int^tL(s)\dd s/s\to\infty\).
& the exact \(t^{-2N}M_{2N-2}(t)\) entropy residual, equivalently the
explicit integrated slowly varying normalization and limiting constant.\\
\hline
Theorem~\ref{thm:main-B}
& \(d\ge1\), \(L\ge d+1\), finite centred \(L\)th absolute moment.
& the displayed functional \(\mathcal R_{d,L}\) satisfies
\(o(t^{-L})\), hence the quotient jet and the large-\(t\) KL representation;
the KL tensor expansion and parity cancellation then follow.\\
\hline
Theorem~\ref{thm:main-B}, stable-kernel clause
& \(d\ge1\), \(0<\alpha<2\), isotropic strictly \(\alpha\)-stable kernel,
finite centred \(p_{\alpha,d}\)th absolute moment.
& the covariance-order stable KL asymptotic; \(p_{\alpha,d}\) is optimal as
a uniform sufficient moment exponent, not asserted necessary for each law
when \(d+\alpha>4\).\\
\hline
Theorem~\ref{thm:main-C}
& finite centred second moment in dimension one.
& compact-window bounded quotient, \(L^1\)-absolute continuity, Cauchy Green
cutoff convergence, local integrability of \(\mathcal I_\nu\), and the tail
identity after the separate endpoint \(H(T)\to0\).\\
\end{tabularx}
}
\end{center}

\subsection*{Convention and organization}

% TODO(major-revision-I6): complete the separate 40--45 page reduction pass by
% moving the order-eight certificate and remaining comparator/bookkeeping
% material to ancillary files; preserve the proved statements during that pass.

The paper uses the extended nonnegative Kullback--Leibler functional of
\eqref{eq:extended-kl-definition}.

Section~\ref{sec:preliminaries} fixes notation.  Sections~\ref{sec:cayley-gate}
and~\ref{sec:haar-pullback} record the Cayley and Haar-pullback
normalizations, including the notation table used in the entropy arguments.
Section~\ref{sec:entropy-asymptotics} proves the coefficient hierarchy, the
variance threshold, and the multidimensional quotient criteria.  It begins
with the detailed proof map, so the introduction need not carry the internal
hypothesis diagram.  Section~\ref{sec:doob-phi-entropy} proves the
compact-window Bregman domain bounds and the Cauchy/KL tail identity.  The
appendices contain the related-work table, normalization details, auxiliary
coefficient certificates, and the proof of the regularly varying
missing-moment boundary layer.

The manuscript itself identifies several “principal” claims, but they are not equal in strength: the one-dimensional variance equivalence is unconditional, while the higher-order moment statement is explicitly only polynomial-domain minimality. The potentially strongest current theorem appears instead to be the sharp stable-kernel moment-class exponent, so I’m checking its exact quantifiers, proof, counterexamples, and the verifier’s objections before choosing the headline.

[32m succeeded in 165ms:[0m
经逐式审读全文并对截至 2026 年 8 月 3 日可检索的原始文献进行排查，最具独立发表潜力的新增内容并非继续计算  $A_8,A_{10},\ldots$ ，而是以下三组定理。其中第三组直接闭合main(960).pdf明确留下的多维  $L^2/KL$  阈值问题。
一、第一 Cayley 模跨尺度完全决定全部商密度
令  $X=\gamma-\bar\gamma$ ，并记

$$a_t(X):=\frac{iX}{2t-iX},\qquad
c_k(t):=\mathbb E[a_t(X)^k].$$

在 Cayley 坐标  $z=(1+iy)/(1-iy)$  下，匹配商密度具有精确 Fourier 表示

$$\frac{(P_t*\nu)(\bar\gamma+ty)}{P_t(ty)}
=
1+\sum_{k\ge1}
\bigl(\overline{c_k(t)}z^k+c_k(t)z^{-k}\bigr).$$

这是精确恒等式，而非渐近展开。其核心结果是所有高阶模态均由第一模态的尺度轨迹生成：

$$\boxed{\;
c_{k+1}(t)=-c_k(t)-\frac{t}{k}c_k'(t),\qquad k\ge1.
\;}$$

因此，单一复值函数  $c_1:(0,\infty)\to\mathbb C$  及其导数已经决定每一固定尺度上的完整 quotient；在有限方差下还精确决定

$$\chi^2(P_t*\nu\|P_t(\cdot-\bar\gamma))
=2\sum_{k\ge1}|c_k(t)|^2,$$

从而决定 KL、文中的非局部 Bregman 耗散以及全部 Laurent 系数。
更强的是，

$$\frac{1+c_1(t)}{2t}
=
\int_0^\infty e^{-2ts}\varphi_X(s)\,ds,
\qquad
\varphi_X(s)=\mathbb E e^{isX}.$$

故第一 Cayley 模的全尺度轨迹是特征函数的 Laplace 变换，因而对概率律  $\nu$  是单射。Cauchy–Stieltjes 变换的唯一性本身属于经典结论；新增之处在于，它恰好等于本文 quotient 的第一圆周模，并满足上述全模态微分闭包。Möbius–wrapped-Cauchy 对应是已有背景，但现有工作未给出这一“单通道完全可观测性”结论；参见 Kato–McCullagh 与 Okamura 的 Cauchy–Stieltjes 刻画。
第一模态还给出一个无尾类假设的完整偶矩层级。若  $\mu_{2j}<\infty$  对  $j<n$ ，则

$$\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&(-1)^{n-1}4^nt^{2n}
\left[
-\Re c_1(t)
-\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}
(-1)^{j-1}\frac{\mu_{2j}}{4^jt^{2j}}
\right]  \\
&\hspace{35mm}
=\mathbb E\!\left[
\frac{X^{2n}}{1+X^2/(4t^2)}
\right]
\uparrow \mathbb E|X|^{2n}.
\end{aligned}}$$

这将本文仅在  $n=1$  得到的无条件方差阈值提升为第一 Cayley 模层面的全偶矩精确层级，而且右端始终非负、单调，不需要正则变化或尾部对称性。
二、Gauss 求积产生无条件的全阶熵阈值
上述模态恒等式允许改变比较对象，从而消除本文高阶熵余项的符号障碍。
1. 两个矩匹配输入的 Poisson–KL 首项
设概率律  $\nu,\eta$  的前  $r-1$  阶矩相同，二者均具有有限  $r$  阶绝对矩，并令

$$\Delta_r=\mu_r(\nu)-\mu_r(\eta)\ne0.$$

则有精确首项

$$\boxed{\;
D_{\rm KL}(P_t*\nu\|P_t*\eta)
=
C_r\Delta_r^2t^{-2r}+o(t^{-2r}),
\qquad
C_r=4^{-r}\binom{2r-2}{r-1}.
\;}$$

常数来自

$$C_r=\frac12\int_{\mathbb R}u_r(y)^2\,\omega(dy),$$

而非本文单参考 KL 多项式中不同模式的混合。证明只需对两个 quotient 作差：

$$\delta_t^\nu-\delta_t^\eta
=
\Delta_r u_r t^{-r}+o(t^{-r}),$$

再使用相对熵在共同正密度附近的二阶 Bregman 展开。由于首项是平方，这一结论不存在本文高阶有符号余项可能发生的抵消。
2.  $n$  点 Gauss 离散律是最优熵压缩
假设  $\mathbb E|X|^{2n-1}<\infty$ ，令  $G_n\nu$  为由  $\nu$  的前  $2n-1$  阶矩确定的  $n$  点 Gauss 求积律。它满足

$$\mu_j(G_n\nu)=\mu_j(\nu),\qquad 0\le j\le 2n-1.$$

若  $\pi_n$  是相对于  $\nu$  的首一  $n$  阶正交多项式，则在  $\mathbb E|X|^{2n}<\infty$  时，

$$\mu_{2n}(\nu)-\mu_{2n}(G_n\nu)
=
\|\pi_n\|_{L^2(\nu)}^2.$$

由此得到

$$\boxed{\;
\lim_{t\to\infty}
t^{4n}D_{\rm KL}(P_t*\nu\|P_t*G_n\nu)
=
4^{-2n}\binom{4n-2}{2n-1}
\|\pi_n\|_{L^2(\nu)}^4.
\;}$$

若  $\mathbb E|X|^{2n}=\infty$ ，则第一模态的正余项恒等式与 Pinsker 有界测试给出

$$\boxed{\;
t^{4n}D_{\rm KL}(P_t*\nu\|P_t*G_n\nu)\longrightarrow+\infty.
\;}$$

因此，在已知有限  $(2n-1)$  阶绝对矩的类上，

$$\boxed{\;
\mathbb E|X|^{2n}<\infty
\iff
\limsup_{t\to\infty}
t^{4n}D_{\rm KL}(P_t*\nu\|P_t*G_n\nu)<\infty.
\;}$$

这是真正的无条件全阶熵阈值。本文主定理 A 正是  $n=1$  的特例，因为  $G_1\nu=\delta_{\bar\gamma}$ 。
此外，Gauss 求积并非任意方便的离散化，而是  $n$  原子比较律中的渐近最优解：任何  $n$  原子律至多匹配到  $2n-1$  阶；若试图再匹配第  $2n$  阶，取其节点多项式  $p(x)=\prod_{j=1}^n(x-x_j)$ ，则原子律下  $\int p^2=0$ ，而非退化  $\nu$  下  $\int p^2>0$ ，产生矛盾。经典 Gauss 求积的最高精度性质可引用 Golub–Welsch，无需在论文中重新证明。
3. 四阶矩的具体新定理
在标准化条件  $\mathbb EX=0,\mathbb EX^2=1$  下，记

$$\beta_3=\mathbb EX^3,\qquad
\pi_2(x)=x^2-\beta_3x-1,\qquad
\kappa=\mathbb E\pi_2(X)^2
=\mathbb EX^4-1-\beta_3^2.$$

令  $G_2\nu$  为对应的二点 Gauss 律，则

$$\boxed{\;
\lim_{t\to\infty}
t^8D_{\rm KL}(P_t*\nu\|P_t*G_2\nu)
=
\frac5{64}\kappa^2.
\;}$$

若  $\mathbb E|X|^3<\infty$  而  $\mathbb EX^4=\infty$ ，则上述归一化量趋于  $+\infty$ 。在对称情形中， $G_2\nu=\frac12(\delta_{-1}+\delta_1)$ ，故

$$\lim_{t\to\infty}
t^8D_{\rm KL}\!\left(
P_t*\nu\,
\middle\|\,
\tfrac12P_t(\cdot-1)+\tfrac12P_t(\cdot+1)
\right)
=
\frac5{64}(\mathbb EX^4-1)^2.$$

这将本文附录 B 中仅为代数缺陷的  $\kappa$  提升为一个实际、非负、具有无条件矩阈值的熵系数。
4. 正则变化边界变成平方律
若  $n\ge2$ ，尾指数为  $-2n$ ，并令

$$M_{2n}(t)=
\mathbb E\bigl[|X|^{2n}\mathbf 1_{\{|X|\le t\}}\bigr]\to\infty,$$

则相对于 Gauss 比较律不再出现本文中的有符号线性边界层，而有

$$\boxed{\;
D_{\rm KL}(P_t*\nu\|P_t*G_n\nu)
\sim
C_{2n}t^{-4n}M_{2n}(t)^2.
\;}$$

在本文的两侧正则变化假设下，

$$M_{2n}(t)\sim 2n(c_++c_-)\ell_L(t),$$

故边界修正为  $\ell_L(t)^2$ ，常数始终为正。这一平方边界律比原稿的系数扣除余项更适合建立高阶统计检验。
三、多维有限协方差存在严格的维数相变
设

$$H_d(t)=
D_{\rm KL}\!\left(
P_t^{(d)}*\nu
\middle\|
P_t^{(d)}(\cdot-\bar\gamma)
\right).$$

1.  $d\le3$ ：有限协方差已经充分且必要
若  $d\le3$ ，则仅需

$$\mathbb E|X|^2<\infty$$

即可证明

$$t^2\delta_t^{(d)}\longrightarrow b_\Sigma
\qquad\text{于 }L^2(\Omega_d),$$

从而由本文定理 3.36 得到

$$\boxed{\;
H_d(t)=Q_d(\Sigma)t^{-4}+o(t^{-4}),
\qquad d\le3.
\;}$$

其中

$$Q_d(\Sigma)
=
c_d^{\rm iso}(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma)^2
+c_d^{\rm tr}\|\Sigma_0\|_{\rm HS}^2$$

正是本文已经计算的协方差二次型。
反向必要性由一维投影和 KL 数据处理不等式得到：径向  $d$  维 Poisson 核的任一一维投影仍是尺度  $t$  的 Cauchy 核；若某一方向二阶矩无限，则本文一维定理 3.18 强制  $t^4H_d(t)\to\infty$ 。因此

$$\boxed{\;
d\le3:\quad
\mathbb E|X|^2<\infty
\iff
\limsup_{t\to\infty}t^4H_d(t)<\infty.
\;}$$

2.  $d\ge4$ ：有限协方差不再控制 KL 首项
当  $d\ge4$  时，存在中心化且具有正定有限协方差的离散概率律，使得

$$\boxed{\;
\limsup_{t\to\infty}t^4H_d(t)=+\infty.
\;}$$

构造可取

$$\nu=(1-W)\delta_0+
\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{w_n}{2}
(\delta_{R_ne_1}+\delta_{-R_ne_1}),
\qquad
w_n=a_nR_n^{-2},
\quad \sum a_n<\infty,$$

再加入任意小的有界全维对称成分以使协方差正定。选择

$$t_n=\kappa R_n w_n^{1/(d+1)}$$

并考察  $R_ne_1$  附近半径  $t_n$  的球。卷积律赋予该球质量  $\gtrsim w_n$ ，而参考 Poisson 核仅赋予质量

$$\lesssim (t_n/R_n)^{d+1}\asymp w_n.$$

经常数分离后，二点数据处理给出  $H_d(t_n)\gtrsim w_n$ ，于是

$$t_n^4H_d(t_n)
\gtrsim
R_n^4w_n^{1+4/(d+1)}
=
a_n^{1+4/(d+1)}
R_n^{\,2(d-3)/(d+1)}.$$

当且仅当  $d>3$  时， $R_n$  的指数为正，可令其沿子序列趋于无穷。这说明维数  $4$  是真实的 KL 相变点，而非证明技术造成的阈值。
3. 精确的  $L^2$  矩阈值
对单个平移 quotient  $F_y(z)$  有

$$\|F_\cdot(z)\|_{L^2(\Omega_d)}^2
\sim K_d|z|^{d+1},
\qquad |z|\to\infty.$$

因此二阶 Taylor 余项的自然增长阶为

$$q_d:=\max\left\{2,\frac{d+1}{2}\right\}.$$

由 Minkowski 不等式和两区域截断可得

$$\mathbb E|X|^{q_d}<\infty
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
t^2\delta_t^{(d)}\to b_\Sigma
\text{ 于 }L^2(\Omega_d).$$

当  $d\ge4$  时，对任意  $2\le p<\frac{d+1}{2}$ ，均可构造有限  $p$  阶矩但上述  $L^2$  收敛失败的中心化尖峰律。因此该阈值在“仅以绝对矩为假设”的全类意义下是尖锐的。
由此形成新的拓扑相图：
拓扑尖锐矩阶 $L^\infty$  quotient $d+1$ ，本文已证明 $L^2(\Omega_d)$  quotient $\max\{2,(d+1)/2\}$ KL， $d\le3$  $2$ KL， $d\ge4$ 严格大于  $2$ ，精确值见下述猜想
4. 最优 KL 阈值猜想
同一尖峰分析给出比  $L^2$  更低的必要矩阶。若权重取  $w_n\asymp R_n^{-p}$ ，则

$$t_n^4H_d(t_n)
\gtrsim
R_n^{\,4-p(d+5)/(d+1)}.$$

因此，对任意

$$2\le p<
p_{\rm KL}(d):=\frac{4(d+1)}{d+5},$$

均存在有限  $p$  阶矩而  $t^4H_d(t)$  无界的概率律。由此得到具有明确证据的尖锐猜想：

$$\boxed{\;
\mathbb E|X|^{\,\max\{2,\,4(d+1)/(d+5)\}}<\infty
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
H_d(t)=Q_d(\Sigma)t^{-4}+o(t^{-4}).
\;}$$

该候选阈值在  $d=3$  恰好等于  $2$ ，并随维数增加趋向  $4$ ，同时严格低于  $L^2$  阈值。这预示 KL、 $L^2$  与  $L^\infty$  三种拓扑具有真正不同的尾部相变。
新颖性与发表价值判断
公开文献中最接近的结果是：


Chen–Niles-Weed 已对 Gaussian smoothing 证明矩匹配条件下的 KL 与  $\chi^2$  渐近，但使用 Hermite 模及指数尾假设，不包含 Cauchy–Poisson 的精确有限矩阈值、Gauss 比较律或维数相变。原始论文


Ma–Wu–Yang 使用矩匹配和 Gauss 求积研究有限 Gaussian mixture 逼近，但不涉及大尺度 Cauchy 混合的精确 KL 常数与无条件矩边界。原始论文


Nielsen–Okamura 处理的是两个有限维参数 Cauchy 律之间的  $f$ -散度，而非一般 Cauchy 平移混合。原始论文


在所检索的原始文献范围内，未发现以下三项已被公开证明：


第一 Cayley 模的全模态微分闭包与全尺度可观测性；


以 Gauss 求积律为比较对象的无条件  $2n$  阶熵阈值及平方正则变化边界；


径向 Poisson–KL 首项在  $d=4$  发生的有限协方差相变。


其中，第二项提供一条完整的新主线，并将本文方差定理解释为  $n=1$  的首层；第三项直接解决原稿明确保留的开放问题。二者分别足以构成独立论文的主定理体系，而第一项应作为统一二者的结构性核心引理。


[32m succeeded in 165ms:[0m
临界充分性成立。关键并非  $L^2(\Omega_d)$ ，而是与 KL 增长及尖峰尺度同时匹配的临界空间

$$q_d:=\frac{d+5}{d+1}\in(1,2),\qquad
p_d:=\frac{4(d+1)}{d+5},\qquad p_dq_d=4.$$

定理（高维 KL 临界矩阈值）
设  $d\ge4$ ， $\nu$  是  $\mathbb R^d$  上的概率律，

$$X=\gamma-\bar\gamma,\qquad \mathbb E|X|^{p_d}<\infty,
\qquad \Sigma=\mathbb E[XX^{\mathsf T}].$$

则

$$D_{\mathrm{KL}}\!\left(P_t^{(d)}*\nu\,\middle\|\,P_t^{(d)}(\cdot-\bar\gamma)\right)
=\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)t^{-4}+o(t^{-4}).$$

其中

$$\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)=\frac12\int_{\mathbb R^d}b_\Sigma(y)^2\,\Omega_d(dy),$$

且

$$b_\Sigma(y)=\frac{d+1}{2}
\left[
-\frac{\operatorname{tr}\Sigma}{1+|y|^2}
+\frac{(d+3)y^{\mathsf T}\Sigma y}{(1+|y|^2)^2}
\right].$$

因此，结合稿件已证明的  $2\le r<p_d$  尖峰反例， $p_d$  是“仅以有限绝对矩表述”的精确 KL 阈值，包括临界端点。
证明
记

$$a=\frac{d+1}{2},\qquad
\Omega_d(dy)=c_d(1+|y|^2)^{-a}\,dy,$$

并定义平移商

$$F_y(z)=\left(\frac{1+|y|^2}{1+|y-z|^2}\right)^a.$$

经平移和尺度变换，

$$1+\delta_t(y)
:=\frac{(P_t^{(d)}*\nu)(\bar\gamma+ty)}
        {P_t^{(d)}(ty)}
=\mathbb E F_y(X/t).$$

1. 临界平移商估计
令  $q=q_d$ 、 $p=p_d$ 。直接计算得

$$\begin{aligned}
\|F_\cdot(z)\|_{L^q(\Omega_d)}^q
&=c_d\int_{\mathbb R^d}
\frac{(1+|y|^2)^{a(q-1)}}
     {(1+|y-z|^2)^{aq}}\,dy .
\end{aligned}$$

置  $u=y-z$ ，并使用

$$1+|u+z|^2\le 2(1+|u|^2)(1+|z|^2),$$

可得

$$\|F_\cdot(z)\|_q^q
\le C_d(1+|z|^2)^{a(q-1)}
      \int_{\mathbb R^d}(1+|u|^2)^{-a}\,du.$$

由于

$$a(q-1)=2,\qquad a>\frac d2,$$

故

$$\boxed{\quad
\|F_\cdot(z)\|_{L^q(\Omega_d)}^q
\le C_d(1+|z|^4).
\quad}                                                    \tag{1}$$

这正是临界四阶尺度；取  $q$  次方根即得到大平移增长率

$$\|F_\cdot(z)\|_q\lesssim |z|^{4/q}=|z|^p.$$

令

$$m=\lfloor p\rfloor
=
\begin{cases}
2,&4\le d\le10,\\
3,&d\ge11,
\end{cases}$$

其中  $d=11$  时  $p=3$ 。记

$$T_m(y,z)=\sum_{|\alpha|\le m}U_\alpha^{(d)}(y)z^\alpha,
\qquad
R_m(y,z)=F_y(z)-T_m(y,z).$$

局部 Taylor 估计给出

$$\|R_m(\cdot,z)\|_q\le C_d|z|^{m+1},
\qquad |z|\le1.$$

另一方面，由 (1)、各  $U_\alpha^{(d)}$  的有界性以及  $m\le p$ ，

$$\|R_m(\cdot,z)\|_q\le C_d|z|^p,
\qquad |z|>1.$$

故全局成立

$$\boxed{\quad
\|R_m(\cdot,z)\|_q\le C_d|z|^p .
\quad}                                                    \tag{2}$$

定义

$$r_t(y):=\mathbb E R_m(y,X/t).$$

由 Minkowski 不等式，

$$\|r_t\|_q
\le \mathbb E\|R_m(\cdot,X/t)\|_q.$$

根据 (2)，

$$t^p\|R_m(\cdot,X/t)\|_q\le C_d|X|^p.$$

而对每个固定  $X$ ，因  $m+1>p$ ，局部 Taylor 估计给出

$$t^p\|R_m(\cdot,X/t)\|_q
\le C_d|X|^{m+1}t^{p-m-1}\longrightarrow0.$$

支配收敛遂得端点余项

$$\boxed{\quad
\|r_t\|_{L^q(\Omega_d)}=o(t^{-p}).
\quad}                                                    \tag{3}$$

这一步只使用临界  $p$  阶矩，不需要任何  $p+\varepsilon$  阶矩。
2. 有界低阶层
由于  $\mathbb EX=0$ ，一次项消失。因此

$$\delta_t=u_t+r_t,$$

其中

$$u_t=
\begin{cases}
t^{-2}b_\Sigma,&m=2,\\[2mm]
t^{-2}b_\Sigma+t^{-3}B_3,&m=3,
\end{cases}$$

而  $B_3$  是由三阶矩构成的有界模式。故

$$\|u_t\|_\infty=O(t^{-2}).                                  \tag{4}$$

此外  $F_y(z)\Omega_d(dy)=P_1^{(d)}(y-z)\,dy$ ，所以

$$\int F_y(z)\,\Omega_d(dy)=1.$$

比较 Taylor 系数可知所有正次数模式的  $\Omega_d$ -积分均为零。
3. 非线性 KL 尾部的端点控制
令

$$\Phi(s)=(1+s)\log(1+s)-s,\qquad s\ge-1.$$

对任意  $1<q<2$ ，存在  $C_q$ ，使得当  $|u|\le1/4$  且  $u+v\ge-1$  时，

$$0\le
\Phi(u+v)-\Phi(u)-\log(1+u)v
\le C_q|v|^q.                                               \tag{5}$$

事实上，当  $|v|\le1/2$  时由  $\Phi''(s)=(1+s)^{-1}$  得到  $O(v^2)$ ，而
 $v^2\le |v|^q$ ；当  $|v|>1/2$  时，利用
 $\Phi(s)=O(1+s^q)$  即得 (5)。该估计同时覆盖  $u+v\downarrow-1$  和任意大的正尖峰。
取  $u=u_t$ 、 $v=r_t$ 。由 (4)，充分大  $t$  时可应用 (5)。Hölder 不等式与 (3) 给出

$$\begin{aligned}
\left|
\int\Phi(\delta_t)\,d\Omega_d
-\int\Phi(u_t)\,d\Omega_d
\right|
&\le
C\|\log(1+u_t)\|_{q'}\|r_t\|_q
  +C\|r_t\|_q^q                                                    \\
&\le
O(t^{-2})\,o(t^{-p})+o(t^{-pq})                                    \\
&=o(t^{-4}),
\end{aligned}                                                     \tag{6}$$

因为  $p>2$  且  $pq=4$ 。
式 (5)–(6) 即为所需的完整非线性 KL 尾控机制。它不要求商一致趋于零，也不把尖峰强行置于  $L^2$ ；任意大的局部密度比均由  $q$ -次增长吸收。
该估计同时证明  $\Phi(\delta_t)\in L^1(\Omega_d)$ 。因此变换公式合法，并且

$$H_d(t)=\int_{\mathbb R^d}\Phi(\delta_t(y))\,\Omega_d(dy).$$

4. 协方差系数
由  $\|u_t\|_\infty=O(t^{-2})$ ，

$$\Phi(u_t)=\frac12u_t^2+O(|u_t|^3)$$

在  $y$  上一致成立。因此

$$\int\Phi(u_t)\,d\Omega_d
=
\frac12t^{-4}\int b_\Sigma^2\,d\Omega_d+o(t^{-4})
=
\mathcal Q_d(\Sigma)t^{-4}+o(t^{-4}).                       \tag{7}$$

结合 (6) 与 (7) 即得定理。
与尖峰必要性的精确闭合
稿件尖峰尺度为

$$t_j=\kappa R_jw_j^{1/(d+1)}.$$

二元数据处理下界具有尺度

$$t_j^4H_d(t_j)
\gtrsim R_j^4w_j^{\,1+4/(d+1)}
=\bigl(w_jR_j^{p_d}\bigr)^{q_d}.                             \tag{8}$$

若仅有  $r<p_d$  阶矩，取  $w_j=\alpha_jR_j^{-r}$  后，(8) 含有正幂

$$R_j^{\,4-rq_d},$$

故快速增长的  $R_j$  产生既有反例。
在临界端点  $r=p_d$  时，该幂严格归零，(8) 退化为

$$\bigl(w_jR_j^{p_d}\bigr)^{q_d}.$$

有限临界矩要求

$$\sum_jw_jR_j^{p_d}<\infty.$$

更重要的是，(1)–(6) 通过 Minkowski 不等式对所有同尺度及不同尺度尖峰作整体控制，尾部代价至多为其临界矩质量的  $q_d$  次方；因此不存在由尖峰聚集产生的额外对数损失。下界中的  $q_d$  与上界中的  $L^{q_d}$ -增长完全相同。
故稿件中的候选指数应提升为精确阈值：

$$\boxed{\displaystyle
p_{\mathrm{KL}}(d)=\frac{4(d+1)}{d+5},\qquad d\ge4,
}$$

且临界端点属于充分侧。

原“同一 Poisson 单元内不可抵消”的断言应全部删除。其严格替代文字仅应作为经典背景说明，不编号、不申报新颖性：

设 P,S 为标准 Borel 空间上的概率测度，(πn​) 为递增有限分割，且
σ(n≥1⋃​πn​)=Bmod(P+S).
则经典相对熵逼近公式给出
D(P∥S)=n→∞lim​D(Pπn​​∥Sπn​​).
固定有限分割仅识别单元总质量，因而可能掩盖子单元上的反号偏差；完整相对熵只能沿生成 Borel σ-代数的细化分割恢复。

严格性与不可删性


严格强于 vague 正则变化。
稿件 Theorem 5.3 的同谱、同 L、同协方差两律分别满足协方差首项成立与聚团子序列发散。由 (6) 可知，其 Gν​ 分别趋零与无界。因此 vague 收敛本身严格不足；此处仅使用稿件既有反例，不另行申报构造。

因而
t4∫Φ(Vtν​)dΩd​≤Ct4−2pd​(logt)2→0.


角向分辨率不可删除。

若该质量均匀分布于球面，则球冠估计给出
tsup​t4∫Φ(Vt,nsph​)dΩd​≤CRn4​an2​→0.
若将同一质量集中于一个方向，则在

两者径向边际完全相同；又因 Bn​=o(L(Rn​))，这些植入不改变 vague 极限。故径向条件或角向平均条件均不充分。


跨壳层聚合必须发生在 Φ 之前。

结论为肯定：存在一个允许原子律、同时聚合角向及跨壳层质量的单一 Poisson 尺度泛函，并且它给出精确充要条件。该泛函不是 Lqd​ 控制量，而是相对于二阶协方差 Poisson 代理的完整非线性 KL 缺陷。
1. Poisson 尺度局部集中泛函
固定 d≥4。设 ν 的均值为 γˉ​，中心化随机向量为 X，协方差为 Σ。令

其无密度、局部可检验形式如下。固定一列嵌套有限 annular–angular 分割

每一项只涉及 Poisson 尺度集合 γˉ​+tA 的概率；因此原始律 ν 不需要具有密度。
2. 充要刻画

t⟼Cν,Σ​(t).

(1+δt​)/(1+ut​)。由凸函数 xlogx 的条件期望逼近，离散 KL 单调收敛到右端，从而证明（2）。

Cν,Σ​(t)≥t4DBer​(pt​(A)∥st​(A)).(6)
因此任何 Poisson 尺度局部聚团均被该泛函强制检测。
3.2 二阶代理的交叉熵

Hd​(t)=DKL​(ht​∥kΣ,t​)+∫Rd​(1+δt​)log(1+ut​)dΩd​.(7)

先有

∥rt​∥L1(Ωd​)​⟶0.(10)
其次成立弱二阶收敛
t2∫bΣ​(y)δt​(y)dΩd​(y)⟶∫bΣ​(y)2dΩd​(y).(11)
为此定义
G(z)=∫bΣ​(y)[Fy​(z)−1−Dz​Fy​(0)z]dΩd​(y).
局部二阶 Taylor 估计及

又有

中心化消去线性项，有限协方差允许支配收敛，故

t2∫bΣ​rt​dΩd​⟶0.(12)
利用

结合（9）即得（8）。
3.3 充要性
将（8）代入（7），并乘以 t4，得到精确缺陷分解（3）。由于
Cν,Σ​(t)≥0,
若 t4Hd​(t)→Qd​(Σ)，则（3）强制
Cν,Σ​(t)→0；这给出必要性。
反之，若 Cν,Σ​(t)→0，则（3）立即给出所需极限。充分方向中的全部非线性尾部均保留在（5）中，未以 Lqd​ 范数或有限阶 Taylor 余项替换。
4. 聚团机制的覆盖范围
该泛函同时处理两类集中：


同一径向壳层内的角向聚团由 annular–angular 分割中的球面小帽检测；（6）给出其严格 Bernoulli KL 下界。


跨壳层聚团不作逐壳层分离，因为
pt,A​=∫[∫A​P1(d)​(y−X/t)dy]νc​(dX)
在进入对数之前已经汇总全部半径和方向的质量。多个壳层在同一 Poisson 单元内叠加时，其质量先相加，再进入离散或连续 KL，因而不能由壳层间抵消规避。


故（4）是所要求的无密度、全方向、跨壳层且完整非线性的充要刻画。

1. Overall assessment
Major revision
The main arguments appear technically credible, and I did not find a direct counterexample to Theorems 3.12, 3.16, 3.18, 3.31, or 4.10. In particular, the bounded-mode proof of the infinite-variance direction in Theorem 3.18 and the regularly varying boundary calculation in Theorem 3.16 are substantial.
The present version is nevertheless not acceptable as written because its headline “sharp moment characterization” conflates two different assertions:


a genuinely analytic characterization at order  $t^{-4}$ ; and


a formally defined domain of evaluation for a universal moment polynomial at higher orders.


For  $m\ge3$ , the displayed iff is true largely because Definition 3.4 defines  $A_{2m}(\nu)\in\mathbb R$  to require every moment occurring in the reduced polynomial to exist. The paper does not prove an analytic iff for the entropy remainder at those orders. This distinction is acknowledged locally but obscured in the title, abstract, introduction, and Main Theorem A.
Theorem C also needs a more candid comparison with general relative- $\Phi$ -entropy dissipation for two distributions evolved by the same symmetric jump semigroup. Its value appears to lie in the finite-variance domain verification and endpoint passage, rather than in the Bregman differentiation formula itself.

2. Novelty ratings
These ratings cover every statement formally labelled “Theorem.” The Main Theorems are composite restatements of later results.

5. Specific improvements needed for acceptance


Make Theorems 3.18 and 3.16 the principal results. They contain the clearest analytic novelty.


Demote Theorem 3.7 to an algebraic minimality proposition unless a genuine higher-order analytic converse is proved.


Reformulate Theorem C as a domain-and-endpoint theorem for the general simultaneous-semigroup relative-entropy identity.


Strengthen Theorem 3.36 by removing its redundant nonlinear assumption.


State precisely that  $L\ge d+1$  is sharp for the chosen  $L^\infty$  quotient topology—not necessarily for existence of the leading KL coefficient.


Replace the manufactured  $R_{d,L}$  hierarchy by a shorter two-region remainder.


Reduce the manuscript substantially. The current 70-page presentation makes elementary coefficient algebra appear coequal with the genuinely new results. A focused version should probably be approximately 40–45 pages, with the order-eight certificate and journal-comparator material separated.


Replace “last-five-years JFA comparators” with a mathematically organized related-work section: convolution moment expansions, stable/fractional entropy dissipation, jump-semigroup Bregman identities, and Cauchy-family divergence formulas.



6. Concrete fixes for BLOCKER and MEDIUM issues
I1 — Correct the status of the higher-order “iff”
Replace the first clause of Main Theorem A by something of the following form:

For  $m\ge2$ , let  $\mathcal A_{2m}\in\mathbb Q[m_2,\ldots,m_{2m-2}]$  be the reduced universal polynomial defined by the constant-term rule. Then

$$\mathcal A_{2m}
=\kappa_m m_2m_{2m-2}
+P_m(m_2,\ldots,m_{2m-3}),
\qquad
\kappa_m=(-1)^m(m-1)2^{-2m+2}\ne0.$$

Consequently  $E|X_c|^{2m-2}<\infty$  is sufficient for evaluating every monomial of  $\mathcal A_{2m}$ , and it is the minimal absolute-moment assumption that guarantees such evaluation uniformly over all centred probability laws.

Then state separately:

$$E X_c^2<\infty
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\limsup_{t\to\infty}t^4H(t)<\infty,$$

which is the genuine analytic iff.
For  $m\ge3$ , the paper already has the appropriate analytic boundary statement inside the regularly varying class:

$$\frac{t^{2m}}{M_{2m-2}(t)}
\left(
H(t)-\sum_{j=2}^{m-1}A_{2j}t^{-2j}
\right)
\longrightarrow \kappa_m\mu_2.$$

This should be advertised as a class-conditional analytic sharpness theorem, not used to suggest an unconditional higher-order iff.
I2 — Place Theorem C within the general jump-semigroup formula
For a symmetric jump generator

$$Lf(x)=\int\bigl(f(y)-f(x)\bigr)J(x,y)\,dy,
\qquad J(x,y)=J(y,x),$$

and two solutions  $\partial_t h_t=Lh_t$ ,  $\partial_t g_t=Lg_t$ , put  $u_t=h_t/g_t$ . The formal identity is

$$-\frac{d}{dt}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(h_t\|g_t)
=
\frac12\iint J(x,y)
\left[
g_t(x)\Lambda(u_t(x),u_t(y))
+
g_t(y)\Lambda(u_t(y),u_t(x))
\right]dx\,dy.$$

By exchanging  $x$  and  $y$  in the second term,

$$-\frac{d}{dt}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(h_t\|g_t)
=
\iint J(x,y)g_t(x)\Lambda(u_t(x),u_t(y))\,dx\,dy.$$

For the Cauchy generator,

$$J(x,y)=\frac1{\pi(x-y)^2},$$

which is exactly the paper’s  $I_\nu(t)$ .
Therefore the theorem’s defensible new content should be stated as:


finite variance implies uniform compact-window upper and lower bounds for  $u_t$ ;


the required noncompact Green pairings are valid;


the Bregman kernel is integrably dominated;


 $H(T)\to0$ , permitting integration to infinity.


The paper should say explicitly whether any cited general theorem already supplies part of this package.
I3 — Remove the redundant hypothesis in Lemma 3.35
Let  $z_t=t^2\delta_t$  and suppose  $z_t\to b$  in  $L^2(\lambda)$ . Define

$$r(s)=
\begin{cases}
\dfrac{\Phi(s)-s^2/2}{s^2},&s\ne0,\\[4pt]
0,&s=0.
\end{cases}$$

For  $\Phi(s)=(1+s)\log(1+s)-s$ ,  $r$  is bounded on  $[-1,\infty)$  and continuous at zero. Then

$$t^4\int\left|\Phi(\delta_t)-\frac12\delta_t^2\right|d\lambda
=
\int
\left|r\!\left(\frac{z_t}{t^2}\right)\right|z_t^2\,d\lambda.$$

Because  $z_t\to b$  in  $L^2$ , the family  $z_t^2$  is uniformly integrable, while  $z_t/t^2\to0$  in probability. Vitali’s theorem therefore yields

$$\int
\left|r\!\left(\frac{z_t}{t^2}\right)\right|z_t^2\,d\lambda
\longrightarrow0.$$

Thus Lemma 3.35 can be strengthened to:

$$t^2\delta_t\to b\quad\text{in }L^2
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\int\Phi(\delta_t)\,d\lambda
=
\frac{t^{-4}}2\int b^2\,d\lambda+o(t^{-4}).$$

No separate nonlinear-integrand hypothesis is needed.
I4 — Establish the sharp scope of  $L\ge d+1$ 
The manuscript can prove that  $L\ge d+1$  is sharp for its uniform quotient topology.
For  $L<d+1$ , choose  $R_n\uparrow\infty$  rapidly and set

$$w_n=R_n^{-L}n^{-2},\qquad
\nu=(1-W)\delta_0+\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{w_n}{2}
\bigl(\delta_{R_ne_1}+\delta_{-R_ne_1}\bigr).$$

Then

$$E|X|^L=\sum_{n\ge1}w_nR_n^L=\sum_{n\ge1}n^{-2}<\infty.$$

Set

$$t_n=8^{-1/(d+1)}
R_n^{(d+1-L)/(d+1)}n^{-2/(d+1)},$$

choosing  $R_n$  so that  $t_n\to\infty$ . At  $x_n=R_ne_1$ ,

$$\frac{(P_{t_n}^{(d)}*\nu)(x_n)}
{P_{t_n}^{(d)}(x_n)}
\ge
\frac{w_n}{2}
\frac{P_{t_n}^{(d)}(0)}
{P_{t_n}^{(d)}(R_ne_1)}
\ge
\frac{w_n}{2}\left(\frac{R_n}{t_n}\right)^{d+1}
=4.$$

Hence  $\|\delta_{t_n}^{(d)}\|_\infty\ge3$ , despite the finite  $L$ -moment. This proves that  $L=d+1$  is the correct threshold for the paper’s uniform quotient strategy.
The authors must then add:

This does not prove that  $d+1$  moments are necessary for the leading KL asymptotic under a weaker  $L^2(\Omega_d)$  or entropy topology.

I5 — Simplify the two-region remainder
For  $L\ge d+1$ , every far-region term in (3.36) is bounded by the top tail term. Replace  $R_{d,L}$  by

$$\widetilde R_{d,L}(t)
=
t^{-(L+1)}
E\!\left[|X_c|^{L+1};\,|X_c|\le t\right]
+
t^{-L}
E\!\left[|X_c|^L;\,|X_c|>t\right].$$

Indeed, for  $d+1\le k\le L$  and  $|X_c|>t$ ,

$$t^{-k}|X_c|^k
\le t^{-L}|X_c|^L.$$

Similarly,

$$t^{-(d+1)}|X_c|^{d+1}
\le t^{-L}|X_c|^L.$$

Thus the existing  $R_{d,L}$  is bounded by a constant depending only on  $d,L$  times  $\widetilde R_{d,L}$ , and

$$E|X_c|^L<\infty
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\widetilde R_{d,L}(t)=o(t^{-L})$$

by dominated convergence and tail integrability. This is shorter and makes clear that the equivalence is a technical encoding of the moment assumption.
I6 — Reorganize the theorem hierarchy
A substantially clearer structure would be:


Theorem A: finite-moment coefficient expansion and Laurent rule.


Theorem B: unconditional variance threshold.


Theorem C: regularly varying boundary residual.


Theorem D: multidimensional quotient/KL expansion.


Proposition: moving Cauchy relative-entropy domain and tail identity.


The current Main Theorem A combines a formal-domain result, an analytic iff, and supporting expansion machinery of very different strength. Splitting these claims would materially improve the paper’s logical precision.
I7 — Replace journal-driven comparison with mathematical comparison
The related-work section should compare formulas, not journal venues. At minimum, it should answer:


Which classical moment-expansion results already yield

$$P_t*\nu=\sum_{|\alpha|\le L}
\frac{(-1)^{|\alpha|}}{\alpha!}\mu_\alpha\,\partial^\alpha P_t
+\text{remainder}?$$



What is new in passing from that density expansion to the matched quotient and then to KL?


Which general relative- $\Phi$ -entropy identity already gives the derivative of  $D_\Phi(P_t\mu\|P_t\eta)$ ?


Which part of the finite-variance domain verification is specific to the Cauchy kernel?


Is the Cayley transform essential to the theorem, or primarily an efficient coefficient-evaluation device?


Answering these questions directly would make the genuine contribution—especially Theorems 3.16 and 3.18—considerably more convincing.
